# Agent records campaign plan

## Goal and exit criterion

Make `@orkestrel/agent`'s two long-conversation strategies durable and honest, give `AgentContext` memos and skills in the package's own vocabulary, gate tool calls with deterministic checks in the loop, and keep the `system` message host-authored, adding nothing the evidence does not carry.

- Fixes: blank-recap refusal, owner-trim order, ledger head pricing, literal vocabulary, the host-authored `system` message (records in the request turn, workspace text in a context message), and the layout vocabulary its reading keeps.
- Capabilities: ledger snapshot and hydrate, correction cap, memo and skill managers wired into the context and the ledger's agent options, Authority `ask` with the `consequential` floor.
- Experiments, each promoted or deleted on its written gate: moving tail, pair narrowing. Anchored recaps stopped at their L0 gate and are deleted unbuilt (O32, O75).
- Measurement: every gate reads on the smallest local model the Study carries, because the smallest models are the most sensitive to these changes and Agent must be usable by them without workarounds (Q5).
- Exit: every unit accepted cheap-first; every experiment switch either promoted (switch deleted, behavior always on) or deleted with its bench; on every path Agent emits exactly one `system` message, built from host inlets only, proven by the canary test; Agent 0.0.35 published with the guide corrected; no `src/` line whose justification is "an experiment might test it".

The campaign builds no second log, no CRDT, no frontmatter parser, no default literal extractor, no core repeat-stop switch, and no guard model.

## Decisions

Each decision is the user's. The user ruled Q1 to Q29 and Q31: Q1 to Q15 on 2026-10-31, Q16 to Q29 on 2026-11-01, and Q31 on 2026-11-02; Q30 is ruled on the user's stated trade-off and listed for the user to veto. The user's standing rules of 2026-11-01 on keeping a measured improvement that misses its target and on the campaign's scope, and their standing word of 2026-11-02 on further runs, are quoted verbatim, recorded, and applied by the Orchestrator rulings O64, O69, and O116.

| Id | Decision | Ruling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q1 | The noun: `Memo` or another word (`Fact` collides with the `fact` category, `Note` with `LedgerNote`) | `Memo` |
| Q2 | Whether the Study compaction arm with the authority repeat rule becomes the compaction baseline | yes; every experiment's off arm runs on 0.0.34, and the 0.0.30 c1 arm is not comparable |
| Q3 | Whether the memo and skill managers ship in Agent 0.0.35 with the `studies/context` mechanism study as their only consumer, the toolbox tools following | yes; the toolbox tools wait for D20. Authority `ask` ships in 0.0.35 as rc-a14 under the user's ruling of 2026-10-31 (O2) |
| Q4 | The GPU allowance in model minutes, and whether the recording extends to goal 77 | left to the Orchestrator: 600 model minutes split per step, and the recording extends to goal 77 (O9, O17, `designs/measurement.md` § GPU allowance) |
| Q5 | Whether a hosted-provider consumer is planned | no; Agent is built for the smallest local models, which expose a design flaw where a larger model works around it (O10, `designs/measurement.md` § Models) |
| Q6 | Whether Agent 0.0.35 may stop a `consequential` tool from running in an application with no approver | yes ("approved on `consequential`"): no fleet tool sets the annotation (F6), and Agent ships no compatibility shim (`@orkestrel/scaffold` `AGENTS.md`, no compatibility shims; `designs/authority.md` § Default authority and the consequential floor) |
| Q7 | Whether a Study release, 0.0.9, carries rc-st1 and, when rc-a9's reading needs it, rc-st2, and when it is published | yes ("work on the release as needed"): the campaign's Study release is 0.0.9, carrying rc-st1 and rc-st3, proven on CPU fixtures and published before rc-a9's first segment; rc-st2 stays conditional and ships in Study 0.0.10, published before the first records screen, when rc-a9's first records segments project a records screen over its cap with a live judge (`designs/runs.md` § Progress) |
| Q8 | Whether each GPU lineage freezes its Node 22 patch: the most recent Node 22 release is read at each lineage start and held to the lineage's last replay, amending O18 for the window | yes (approved), because positional replay refuses any other Node release, a patch included (`designs/runs.md` § Replay and pins) |
| Q9 | Whether each GPU lineage freezes its Ollama version: the most recent stable release is screened at a lineage start, and every arm of a gate runs under one daemon pin that the profile `daemon` member makes Study verify | yes (approved) (`designs/runs.md` § Replay and pins) |
| Q10 | Whether one CPU test lane may run beside a GPU segment | no (D47; the user ruled as recommended); if granted, only for gates that read tokens and labels, never rc-a3 or rc-a11, after rc-p4 reads within 5% of quiet |
| Q11 | Whether `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.agents/orchestration.md` § tmp layout gains one row for `tmp/studies/`: the results, closures, gradings, labels, scratch, and run records the Study executable writes, and study data a consumer keeps out of its tracked tree; never swept | approved ("Q11 approved", 2026-10-31); the scaffold session ships the row, and nothing in Agent waits on it |
| Q12 | Whether a deny rule on reading, searching, and listing the answer key's untracked copy and its historical path, the first mechanical guard of `tenets.md` § Evaluation's never-read line (O27), lives in the user-level settings file | approved 2026-10-31 and applied in the user-level settings file, whose `permissions.deny` holds `Read(**/tmp/studies/private/**)` and `Read(**/studies/endurance/sources/private/**)`, so a checkout at an older commit is covered too; a read of a file under either folder, and a shell command whose text names a path under either, was refused when checked on 2026-10-31, while a command naming only the folder as an exclusion pathspec runs, so a command that must name a file under either folder runs from a script file and its calling command names only the script; this repository's `.claude/settings.json` is `@orkestrel/scaffold`'s copy, and a `settings.local.json` would be an ignored file outside `tmp/` (O29); a command-text rule on `git show` is no boundary, so the tenet stays the guard there |
| Q13 | The browser and server boundary, round 1: whether 0.0.35 refuses a stored `system` role at every ingress (D33); whether one `Agent` shared across concurrent requests is guarded or only documented; who owns the system turn a relay forwards; and whether the page-to-Node placement is named with a wire contract | refuse the stored role: "Refuse it now (Recommended)"; the shared agent: "Let's think this one through, I feel that might be the point is to be able to switch in and out on a dime, but if it means a security issue or if it weakens the agent or makes it brittle instead of helping with freshness then think it through"; the relay: "Not sure but I would say the server should always win but if the user wants the server to accept a system prompt then let them but explicitly otherwise the page to server prompt can either silently drop or explicitly error out, but the point is to be explicit between them."; the placement: "Name it and ship a contract" (O34, O37) |

## Rulings

### Verified facts

- F1. The Ollama judge sends one request per question: the `OllamaJudge` remarks in `@orkestrel/ollama` 0.0.27 read "Each question uses a separate non-streaming generate request." Grouping a message's asks saves zero wire calls, so the grouped-asks experiment (the subjective proposal's E3) is refused.
- F2. The owner-record trim drops the most recent lines first: `Ledger#plan` builds owner-record steps from `record.lines` in conversation order, and each `Ledger#trim` step replaces the keyed record's lines with `record.lines.slice(0, -1)` (`src/core/ledgers/Ledger.ts`, as of `1e82d145`). The rules record survives only because `selectRecords` moves on-topic rules lines ahead of off-topic ones. rc-u5 is a fix, test first.
- F3. `Message.id` never reaches the Ollama wire: `mapMessages` in `@orkestrel/ollama` 0.0.27 sends `role`, `content`, `tool_calls`, and `images` only. The system-message id that `AgentContext#build` mints on every call leaves Ollama prefix reuse intact. Probe rc-p1 (c) is dropped, and D17 closes.
- F4. `@orkestrel/tool` declares `ToolAnnotations { pure?, untrusted?, consequential? }`: `untrusted` "Reports that the tool's value can carry content the tool did not author"; `consequential` "Reports that running the tool has a consequence a caller must confirm" (`ToolAnnotations` in `@orkestrel/tool`, https://github.com/orkestrel/tool).
- F5. `@orkestrel/mcp` maps `consequential` to MCP `destructiveHint` and to WebMCP `consequentialHint` (`toolAnnotationsToMCP` in `src/core/helpers.ts` and the browser mapping in `src/browser/helpers.ts`, https://github.com/orkestrel/mcp).
- F6. No `@orkestrel/toolbox` source sets `untrusted` or `consequential` (https://github.com/orkestrel/toolbox).
- F7. `@orkestrel/ollama`'s `tests/service/authority.test.ts` builds decisions with `allowed`, so its re-pin migrates that file (https://github.com/orkestrel/ollama).
- F8. `ToolManager#execute` in `@orkestrel/tool` starts a batch with `Promise.all`, so rc-a14 rebuilds the allowed map in call order.
- F9. `raceSignal` exists (`src/core/conversations/helpers.ts`, as of `2da6d0b6`): it removes its abort listener on settle and absorbs a late rejection, so the ask pass uses it and adds no abort helper. `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/patterns.md` § Event maps forbids a generic status event, so decisions publish as `allow`, `ask`, and `deny`.

### Orchestrator rulings

- O1. Adopt the consultation's decision, reconciliation, records, compaction, memos and skills, context and machinery, measurement, sequence, and deferrals, with F1 to F3 applied. The `designs/` files hold that substance.
- O2 (restated 2026-10-31). `remember` never ships without `ask`, and `ask` ships first, in Agent 0.0.35 as rc-a14; D6 closes. The user ruled on 2026-10-31: "we still need to address Authority and ask ... security really has become an issue with models and I want to make sure we have that integrated and ready to work as soon as possible and working well."
- O3. Route each `astra` unit to the objective bench when the roster lists that bench as available; when it does not, the unit runs on the subjective lane's engine and the carrying commit records the substitution.
- O4. The noun is `Memo` (Q1).
- O5. Stop line: after Agent 0.0.34 is served and pushed, the campaign runs every unit from S1 through S8 without further user input, each GPU step inside its cap (O9). It stops and reports before publishing Agent 0.0.35, because the user owns every publication (`tenets.md` § Release) and every push except that of a release the registry confirms (O31). It also stops and reports when the GPU allowance is spent.
- O6. Estimate: no measurement of these units exists. The closest is the prior campaign's R2 CPU proof, which ran 327 of 335 implementation minutes for one medium unit (`152ac3c3:.orkestrel/agent/r2-u41-ruling.md`). Each unit brief states its own estimate and stop cost.
- O7. The closed fleet records `.orkestrel/plan.md`, `.orkestrel/ledger.md`, `.orkestrel/release.md`, and `.orkestrel/scaffold/release-audit-verdict.md` record closed campaigns (fleet 2026-10-17, Mechanisms, Recording, the 2026-10-24 release, the boost campaign), and the fleet wave confirmed on 2026-10-30 that it reads none of them. The records commit deletes them per `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/campaign.md` § Hand off and close; git history keeps them.
- O8. These records are committed by explicit pathspecs; the first records commit, `30c8cc71` on 2026-10-31, left the uncommitted tarball pins in `package.json` and `package-lock.json` out of the commit. Nothing in this campaign pushed Agent's `main` before `main` was pushed through `fe5a1703`, Agent 0.0.34's release commit, after the registry served 0.0.34.
- O9. GPU allowance (Q4): 600 model minutes, the summed `total_duration` of every agent and judge call, split into the per-step caps of `designs/measurement.md` § GPU allowance. A cap stop is a budget stop, never a quality verdict (`tenets.md` § Scope). The recording extends to goal 49 on both arms and to goal 77 on the records arm.
- O10 (amended 2026-10-31). Subject model (Q5): every GPU gate reads on Qwen 3.5 2B, the smallest model with prior readings; the records judge stays Mica v0.1 4B; an experiment that promotes passes one non-inferiority pair on Qwen 3.5 4B (`designs/measurement.md` § Models). The user confirmed the 2B subject on 2026-10-31: "Sounds good, we can start with the 2b model."
- O11. The `system` message carries host-authored text only: nothing a user wrote, a tool returned, a judge or summarizer produced, or a model wrote, a model-written memo included. Agent's inlets to it are the system text, instructions with loaded skill bodies, the skill index, and host-registered tool definitions; the tools block renders inside the `system` turn (`designs/layout.md` § Boundary). The user ruled on 2026-10-31: "we need to be careful and make sure that nothing gets into it from the user like we found that it has, and also after it we need to make sure that we have the proper order of text that goes to the agent from the context and in ways that the agent can digest it effectively."
- O12. The layout move, rc-u10, is a fix with no switch. Its cost is read one time, by rc-a10 for rc-u10 against a Study transport rewrite that rebuilds the 0.0.34 placement; that arm is study-only and deleted after the screen, and no screen returns records to `system` in what Agent ships. Amended by Q30's ruling of 2026-11-02: that read is one time or recorded unread, and the rewrite is deleted either way (`designs/measurement.md` § Screens).
- O13. The Study pins the effective sampling values on every role; a value change is D38 (`designs/layout.md` § Sampling).
- O14. Adopt the security consultation, with F4 to F9 and C32 to C41 applied; `designs/authority.md` holds that substance. Its ruling that lookup readings stay in the `system` message and its form of D25 are superseded by O11 and O15.
- O15. Adopt the layout consultation, with C42 to C47 applied; `designs/layout.md` holds that substance, and § Sequence holds its merged unit table.
- O16. `AuthorityInterface.ask` is optional, and the `Authority` class always implements it. A later move to a required `ask` is breaking; the reverse is not.
- O17. The GPU allowance stays 600 model minutes, split again per `designs/measurement.md` § GPU allowance: rc-a6's 90 minutes fund rc-a16 (15), rc-a10 for rc-u10 (45), and its contingency (30); rc-a15 takes 10 from the larger-model checks, which keep 100.
- O18. Node line (the user, 2026-10-31: "Let's have everything run on the latest 22 version going forward unless the package requires 24."): every check, gate, probe, and study run of this campaign runs on the newest Node 22 release at the start of the run, recorded in its run record, and on the newest Node 24 release at the start of the run, recorded in its run record, only for a package whose manifest requires 24. The declared support floor stays `engines` `>=22.18.0`.
- O19 (amended 2026-10-31). Run shape, on the user's ruling of 2026-10-31 that no model run is ridden out (`designs/runs.md` § Ruling): every GPU execution is one arm and one sealed segment, run from a `study freeze` closure, started from an end-of-goal checkpoint a sealed earlier segment staged (a lineage's first segment from goal 1), and projected at 15 model minutes or less from the last sealed segment's measured rates; the first segment of rc-a9's lineage holds 2 goals, the second 6, and a screen lineage opens with its `on` arm's first segment of `designs/runs.md` § Segment plan. Each decision other than a Study ceiling is taken at a segment boundary by launching or not launching the next segment. A step's cap is enforced by projection before launch; the `record` allowances sit at twice the projection as a backstop that must never bind, because a `record` refusal leaves the result interrupted (Study `guides/study.md` § Native command, the recording-allowance paragraph; `designs/runs.md` § Run shape).
- O20. Stops: Study ceilings and Agent ceilings keep their layers; an invariant failure (more than one `system` message or one not at index 0, a missing or changed pinned sampling key, `think` not false, a model digest off the pin, a closure fingerprint or Node runtime differing across a gate's arms) ends the chain as "measurement invalid"; curtailment ends only a treatment arm, only on a count clause the gate has already lost, only after its control arms sealed; every other signal is an alert read at the boundary. No in-process quality guard ships in the first lineage (D39; `designs/runs.md` § Stops).
- O21 (amended 2026-10-31). Progress: a progress reader parses `exchanges.jsonl`, `evidence.jsonl`, and `attempt.json` by byte offset at segment completions only. The reader is Study's `study progress` command (rc-st1), in Study 0.0.9 served before rc-a9's first segment, and it calls the memory adapter's `progress` (`StudyMemoryAdapter#progress`, rc-a17) per listed goal. rc-p3 is retired and its id never returns. The term is `progress` (`designs/runs.md` § Progress).
- O22. Fixes while a step runs climb the ladder fixture, hydrate, replay, request, micro, screen (`designs/runs.md` § Fixes while a step runs). Writers edit and run owned-file format, lint, and type checks beside a live segment; tests, builds, offline replays, and other model work wait for the boundary. § Parallelism carries the GPU line.
- O23. The `rc-o` prefix the run-operations designs proposed is not used: the probes are rc-p2 to rc-p4, the seed and checkpoint instrument is rc-a17, and the Study units are rc-st1 and rc-st2. The README id law admits any `rc-` id and names the `rc-st` Study kind.
- O24. The `q2` profile's goal call bound is 512: bursts average about 137 judge calls (`research/evidence.md` § Prior-campaign readings, judge bursts), and a 128-call goal bound was exhausted at goal 49 in the prior campaign (§ Prior-campaign readings there, goal call bound). Its sampling, `record`, `observe`, and `daemon` members follow `designs/runs.md` § Run shape and rc-s1's first change in § Sequence, and its every-goal checkpoint list follows rc-a17 there.
- O25 (amended 2026-10-31). The GPU table keeps O17's caps (sum 600) with the segment plan of `designs/runs.md` § Segment plan. When a screen's projection exceeds its cap with a live judge, take the judge saving from rc-st2 when Study 0.0.10 is served (Q7), else cut goals per arm and report the stratum reached; the contingency and larger-model rows stay reserves.
- O26 (amended 2026-10-31). Studies layout, adopting the studies-organization consultation (rc-consult5) on the user's ruling of 2026-10-31, "make the studies folder nice and organized and any tmp folder we should come up with for generated stuff we shouldn't need to track", as the naming consultation (rc-consult6) amends it under O29. A declaration folder holds only `study.json` and `rubric.json`. `studies/capacity` and `studies/qualification` keep their names: `capacity` is the capacity twin of `memory` over `handoff`, `qualification` is the capacity twin of `endurance` and produces the result the profile member `qualification` names, and no single word replaces either without inventing a term. `studies/memory/` holds the driver code, `profiles/` (`local`, `q4`, `q2`, and one single-word profile per screen arm or experiment switch as it lands), `scenarios/`, `provenance/{endurance,handoff}.json`, and `sources/{README.md,manifest.json,evaluation.json,development.json}`; the README states custody facts only, and `provenance/endurance.json` alone holds the rubric's derivation. `studies/memory/profiles/q4-room.json` is removed, and the `q4` profile's `qualification` member names `tmp/studies/qualification`. Results, closures, gradings, labels, and run records live under `tmp/studies/`; no generated file sits under the tracked tree, and no `.gitignore` line is added. rc-s2's `studies/context/` enters as one declaration over the `tools` driver: `study.json` and `rubric.json`, its `libraries` scenario under `studies/tools/scenarios/`, the shared `local` profile, and `tests/studies/context/integration.test.ts`. rc-u12 carries the move.
- O27. Answer key: no live unit, test, script, or configuration reads the three files under `studies/endurance/sources/private/`; the custody rule bars them from every prompt, query, label, and selection rule; the rubric derived from the evaluation oracle is tracked on its own; and lane searches of the tracked `studies/` reached that directory (the search deviations of `ledger.md`). The files leave the tracked tree by the Orchestrator's directory rename to `tmp/studies/private/endurance/`, with their bytes unread and never copied, before rc-u12's builder starts; on a rename refusal the Orchestrator stops and reports and never copies. `studies/memory/sources/manifest.json` stays tracked and keeps their SHA-256s, and git history holds them at rc-u12's parent commit, `64da0a29`. `tenets.md` § Evaluation's never-read line names the history location and the untracked copy. No history rewrite.
- O28. Sequence: rc-u12 runs in the runner lane right after rc-u1 and rc-u2 are accepted and committed, before rc-p1, rc-a8, rc-a14, and any later baseline that reads `studies/` or `tests/studies/`. This departs from the consultation's "before rc-u1 and rc-u2": those two units read and write no file under `studies/` or `tests/studies/`, and their baselines (2026-10-31, `f8ea2662`) were taken on the unchanged tree.
- O29 (amended 2026-10-31). The guide names what a per-principal `Agent` shares across its threads: the workspace pointer, the scope, instructions, memos, the emitter, and `agent.abort()`. Each session entry builds its own `window`, `budget`, and `allowance`, never shared across principals, and while one run is live an `Agent` built with any of them refuses that principal's next run on any thread with `AgentError` `'CONCURRENCY'` (409 on the wire, a rethrow in Node) rather than queue it, so the host bounds a run it cannot finish; the application owns the entry's lifetime and eviction. rc-d1's inlet table binds the thread id to the principal and keys a durable store by principal (§ Units, row rc-d1). Each fence transcription adds two cases: two principals posting one thread id get distinct conversations, and a same-principal overlap on one thread answers 409 (wire) or rethrows (Node) with the thread's message count unchanged. Each fence keeps the message `thread.add` returns, wraps only the synchronous `stream` or `generate` call, calls `thread.remove(message.id)` on any synchronous throw, then answers 409 for `AgentError` `'CONCURRENCY'` (wire) or rethrows (Node), and removes nothing on an asynchronous rejection (`designs/boundary.md` § Run binding); a run that appends its own user turn is D64. The frame route composes the same origin and CSRF checks as the answer route before it reads the body (`designs/boundary.md` § Page wire, Route pattern). rc-u16 launches after rc-u14 and rc-u15 both commit (the Orchestrator's launch condition, 2026-11-01), superseding O44's "rc-u15 can continue beside rc-u16"; rc-u15 can continue beside rc-u6. A refusal ends the stream: the refusing call returns nothing, discards every line it holds before and after the refused one plus the partial line, and leaves the parser as `clear()` leaves it, so a caller that keeps feeding gets another `'SYNTAX'` on the dropped line's remainder, never a spliced record; the guide's opening paragraph says the lines after the refused line in the same call are discarded, and the `strict` sentence sits in the `NDJSONParserOptions` remarks. `errors.test.ts` stays, because the guard is logic; the deep-nesting case probes the host engine and asserts against the measured result; the distribution case that needs the registry can skip before the release and is required under `--mode release`; Signature cells are copied from the `test:guides` output. rc-nd1 launches with rc-u14 and rc-u15 on the `ad866d2b` and ndjson `699230c` baselines, as the one writer in the `@orkestrel/ndjson` checkout; it runs only its owned-file format check, non-fix lint, and scoped type checks, and the one runner lane runs every test, mutation, and gate. Strict mode trims each line with `String.prototype.trim` in both modes, with no untrimmed strict variant: rc-nd1's trim case iterates the ECMAScript whitespace set and the line terminators a split line can hold, each row written with `\u{…}` escapes on its own strict parser, and rc-u17's default-refusal case places the trimmed settled-result line after an earlier complete line, because `TextDecoder` strips a byte-order mark at byte 0; the guide says the change is Unicode space around a line inside the stream. The `'PROTOCOL'` translation is framing-agnostic: private `#parse(parser, chunk, combined)` and `#finish(parser, combined)` in `AgentProvider`, called where `parser.parse` and `this.finish(parser)` run, rethrow the error unchanged when `combined.aborted`, `isProviderError(error)`, or `isProviderAbortError(error)` holds (brand guards, never `instanceof`) and otherwise throw `ProviderError('PROTOCOL', 'provider error: malformed wire record', { cause: error })`; neither encloses `this.read(record)`, `#emit`, or the loop body; `#translate` and its lead comment stay; `AgentProvider.ts` imports nothing from `@orkestrel/ndjson`; the class remarks gain the framing-seam sentence and the guide's `'PROTOCOL'` bullet covers a line the framing refuses. Its cases: a plain `Error` from `parse` or `finish` becomes `'PROTOCOL'` with that error as `cause`, a `ProviderError` `'PROVIDER'` from a custom parser passes through, `isNDJSONError(error.cause)` holds on the default relay path, and the mutation deletes the wrap. A pin route packs the `Release 0.0.15` commit from a clean ndjson HEAD, never rc-nd1's acceptance commit. rc-u17 launches after rc-u15 and rc-u16 both commit, and rc-u16 keeps its ruled page framing; in the commit that retires `RelayFrameParser`, rc-u17 moves rc-u16's page fence to one `createNDJSONParser({ strict: true })` per body with `parser.parse('\n')` at its end and a per-record `agentFrameContract.is` check, treats a thrown `NDJSONError`, a refused record, and a body that ends without `finish` or `error` as protocol errors, adds a transcription case (a malformed line, a non-object line, a non-frame record, and an unterminated trailing line) and a mutation (the page fence on the lenient parser fails it), and extends its launch-HEAD census with `agentFrameContract` and `parseJSONAs(line`. Both server fences keep removing the user turn only on a synchronous throw and add no tail check, because a tail check needs both exits (a cancel or a run timeout resolves) and erases an ask whose partial answer the page might already show; `designs/boundary.md` § Run binding records the rejection, and D64 is the route that closes the gap. The kept tail is a stated limit with an executed proof: rc-u14 adds a server-section case (a scripted provider that rejects before the first append; `handle` rejects; the tail is the appended user message; a second `handle` on the same thread appends after it; and the second run's recorded request carries both user messages adjacent and in order), rc-u16 extends its cancel-during-ask case the same way, and each lands its sentence with its case: a run that rejects, aborts, or times out before any assistant append leaves the user turn as the thread's tail; the next request carries it unanswered directly before the following turn, so the model can answer it or call tools for it, and a call the authority allows outright runs without asking; an application that wants a cancelled ask withdrawn calls `thread.remove(message.id)` after settle while that message is still the tail. `designs/boundary.md` § Page wire dates its "no SSE or NDJSON package" clause at `ad866d2b` and names rc-u17's dependency. rc-u17 runs late, with no interim Ollama or Study release: it launches after the campaign's last Study command that loads a profile's Ollama provider entry (every execute and every offline or keyed replay under `q2`, `q4`, and each rc-s1 per-arm profile, the `q4` larger-model checks of `designs/runs.md` included) and after every unit whose acceptance names `test:studies` (rc-s1's last profile change or re-pin, and every strip or removal unit for an experiment switch or profile), immediately before rc-d1 in S8. Its criterion 0 is that `npm ls @orkestrel/ndjson --all` lists 0.0.15 at the top level and exactly one nested 0.0.14 under `@orkestrel/ollama`, and nothing else; `study check` is no evidence for the nested copy, because `check` excludes the provider entry. The known red window starts at the install that puts `@orkestrel/ndjson` 0.0.15 in Agent's tree during rc-u17 (on the registry route, the install before its dependency commit; on the pin route, the install after the restore) and lasts until Agent's closing development re-pin. § Release holds the release order, the known red case, the consumer migration, and the closing re-pin condition this ruling sets.

- O53. Measurement method (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on a measurement plan, a host survey, a research lane, one read-only consultation, and one refuter, adopting the consultation's amendments and the refuter's). The user delegated the measurements on 2026-11-01: "I'm leaving most of the measurements up to you, since they are subjective and difficult for me to gauge except as a user of the packages in an application. I need you to thoroughly research these measurements and also check the computer we are using and make sure that they are reasonable measurements while also still trying to get the best performance we can." Thresholds and methods are therefore Orchestrator rulings, and only scope and product behavior go to the user. Every gated reading from rc-a3b on follows this method, which `designs/measurement.md` § Method holds in full.
  - Statistic: wall time is primary; CPU is read only where a cell's median is at least 500 ms, because the CPU timer's quantum is 16 ms. One process per arm per cell, arms interleaved, garbage collection unforced, warmups before measured samples, and tinybench with `time: 0`, `iterations: n`, and `warmup: false`. Every ratio gate at or under 1.10 runs at least 2 processes per arm, interleaved A B B A, and every 1.05 gate at least 3, because the t multiple at 2 processes, 4.30, leaves a 1.05 gate about 1% of margin; at 5 or fewer processes, a t interval on process medians replaces a cluster bootstrap. A ratio gate reads both builds interleaved in one slot, the before build from a copy the Orchestrator takes at that slot's start beside the after build and the A/A copy; an earlier before reading is a trigger reading, a cross-check, or a sensitivity control only (`designs/measurement.md` § Method, MM-6).
  - Controls: an A/A bound at 2 to 2.5 times the measured between-arm noise, a slot whose A/A trips reported as unreadable, apart from pass and fail; a sensitivity control that injects the threshold plus 3 times that noise and also the threshold itself, which reads at least marginal; and before any gate at or under 5% is set, one A/A pilot of at least 6 processes on the 1600-message seed fixes the between-process noise and rewrites the detectability table; the pilot runs over the control `select` first in rc-a3b's before slot, and rc-u19's first measurement repeats it over the correction `select` (O54).
  - Host preconditions per slot: no `node`, `npm`, `tsc`, `vitest`, or `oxlint` process outside the runner's tree with a CPU-time delta over 100 ms across the slot, read from the process list at slot start and end, each such process present before the first slot, the IDE's background daemon, and the network service recorded by command line as known background under their own bound (`designs/measurement.md` § Method, HP-1), and a slot whose A/A or outlier rule trips rerun; GPU memory in use at or under 100 MiB and the Ollama process count at the daemon plus its tray, read at slot start, at slot end, and per cell, never contacting a daemon; other load at most 6.25% over a 10 s quiet baseline before the first cell and at most 10% per cell, as hard bounds, with 3% as the aim, a cell over 10% rerun one time and a second miss reading `contested`; a reference kernel (a 200 ms spin-and-sort) at slot start and end within 3%; the power plan recorded by its identifier; identical V8 flags across arms, with `--max-old-space-size` unset.
  - Thresholds: rc-a3b's gates read wall time with CPU beside, and a control non-regression gate joins them, the after build's control arm at 1600 messages at most 1.05 times the before build's (O54 sets rc-a3b's own acceptance); rc-u21's cost trigger falls to 12.5 ms under the page rule at the end of this bullet, its slope trigger needs at least 4 ms at 20000 deltas, the smallest page-path gain worth having, so it fires on growth before the total reaches 12.5 ms, its pace-keeping case reads within 1.05 times, and it reports the largest single append at 20k beside the total; rc-u20 reports the 1804-judgment control seed's hydrate at most 1.05 times beside its gate, and its upstream guard sites join at 12.5 ms under the same rule; rc-u19's drop is at least 3% and at least 16 ms, read on the frozen rc-a3 seed for both builds, and rc-u19 is not built when rc-a3b's after-run correction arm reads under about 530 ms; rc-ct1 keeps 20%, its A/A built from two separate copies of the HEAD build and bounded under the method's A/A rule; the floor of Q26 stands as the user ruled it, the control arm's `select` CPU at 1600 messages with wall time beside, its slope on the span from 800 to 1600 messages; after rc-a9, the reading at the realistic size rc-a9's report names and `select` wall as a share of the 2B time to first token rc-a9 reads are reported beside it, and gating the floor there comes to the user as a proposal, because it changes Q26's basis; every 1.05 non-regression gate runs at least 3 processes per arm; every `src/core` path that can run in a page uses the page budget divided by a 4x device factor, 12.5 ms on the measuring host, the factor's source recorded in O55.

The review found that a reader suspended at its `yield` held its taken cell, whose `next` chain kept every later cell reachable, a retention the copied array never had; the drain loop reads the value and moves `#head` before the `yield`, so no cell outlives its take in a reader's frame, the remark states the bound (a channel retains at most one taken value, and a suspended reader holds only the value it took), the mutation files follow the loop, and the after build was recaptured. - O64. The keep rule (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, recording and applying the user's standing rule of 2026-11-01: "If something misses a target by a bit but still had better performance, we should keep it and not revert it.", read against `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Performance on the records check of the wave 1 apply). A change is kept when its measurement shows it faster on the case it targets, the interval clearing no change in the faster direction, even when the point misses its improvement target, and the miss is recorded as read, with no bar moved after the data. A reading that shows no gain, its interval including no change, reads not shown, and keeps the change only when its brief names a merit beyond speed before the run: correctness, bounded retention, or a simpler reading for a developer or a small model. A pure speed change whose gain is not shown reverts, because `quality.md` § Performance refuses a micro optimization without its own measured gain. A change that makes a case users hit slower beyond its no-regression bar reverts in every case, and a mixed change, faster in one case and slower beyond the bar in another, reverts or goes to the user, as rc-ct1's did (Q28, O62). `tenets.md` § Scope holds the rule, and `designs/measurement.md` § Method, MM-18 states how a brief applies it. Every brief not yet launched (rc-u19, rc-u20, rc-u6, rc-u18, rc-a18, and every later performance unit) has its miss and revert clauses rewritten to this rule in a brief-2 before launch; O56 already applies it to rc-a3b.
- O65. No local adoption of rc-ct1's walk (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on the ct1 local-adoption lanes, a read-only consultation, and one refuter per proposal). No site in Agent or a related package adopts the span walk, and contract's source stays at the 0.0.21 bytes with the pins and the bench of `09d0673`. The mechanism, read from the builds and the slot records and not ablated: the walk saves about 34 ns per step (no generator resumption, getter and closure, `Reflect.apply` with a fresh array, or second result object) and adds about 0.43 to 0.51 µs of setup per loop, so it wins only on long walks over cheap elements; the crossover, about 13 to 15 steps per loop, is inferred from the measured loss (1 to 16 entries, 5 to 8 keys) and gain (1000 or more), not measured. Agent holds no array that is both large and cheap-element: its large arrays (messages, sections, judgments) run heavy element guards, and the 51000-judgment pool through a prebuilt `recordOf`, the analogue of Agent's judgment hydrate, read 19% slower. In Agent, an index loop is refused by `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/typescript.md` § Bindings and a local walk variant by `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Ecosystem reuse, because it carries no semantic difference, and it would drop contract's pinned-prototype guarantee. The fleet survey found no hot site that runs a container guard over a large cheap-element array. A later contract revisit first adds size cells of 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, and 128 to `tests/src/core/combinators.bench.ts` and reads the crossover in one interleaved process pair against the released build and the rc-ct1 candidate build, kept untracked in the contract checkout's probe directory, or a later candidate. A figure carried from the contract bench to Agent illustrates and is never an Agent measurement.
- O66. rc-u20 is the lever (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on the ct1 local-adoption round). The cost is guard construction per call, not the walk: `isJudgment`, `isSection`, and `isMemo` build a `recordOf` or `objectOf` on every call (U3, about 7 µs per construction under the profiler, an upper bound), and rc-u20 owns the hoist. rc-u20 launches after rc-u19 commits, only on a brief-2 written to O64 and checked: a hydrate drop on the correction seed that its interval shows, short of 25% or 250 ms, is kept with the miss recorded; a drop that is not shown reverts, because the hoist is a pure speed change, unless the brief-2 names a merit beyond speed before the run; the control seed's hydrate over 1.05 times before reverts a site in every case; and its write trigger takes its own reason or is dropped. No gain figure is cited for rc-u20 except its own before and after readings. A hydrate reading folded into rc-a18 is dropped, because rc-a18's profile reads only marked regions, every hydrate falls outside them, and its slot is not ordered after rc-u20.
- O67. The duplicate hydrate passes (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on the ct1 local-adoption round). Agent's ledger hydrate validates each judgment three times and each message twice; collapsing the passes is deferred as D76, with its trigger, its target, and its revert bar (§ Deferred).

D72's trigger is restated: rc-ct1 closed reverted, so D72's follow-ups run on their own bench trigger alone, never in a unit that changes the guard walk.

The campaign reads and records Agent and its related packages only: its runtime and development dependencies, `@orkestrel/contract` and `@orkestrel/ndjson` among them, its dependents `@orkestrel/ollama`, `@orkestrel/toolbox`, and `@orkestrel/study`, and the `@orkestrel/mcp` fixture of § Release; no lane reads an unrelated package, and no record carries an advisory for one. - O70. rc-u19's re-evaluation (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on one read-only consultation with proposals P1 to P9 and one refuter per proposal, each holding with an amendment). P1 to P6 and P9 are adopted, each with its refuter's amendment, and P7 and P8 stay rejected with their amended reasons; where an amendment and a proposal differ, the amendment governs, and where two amendments differ, rc-u19's brief-2 takes the stricter reading and says so. rc-u19 launches as sequenced, beside rc-u6 on disjoint files, after the records commit (O59); the unit and its breaking entry are already planned (O55, § Release), so no user question is raised.
  - Premise: rc-a3b cut both the wall and the copy count, because its memos in `Classifier#gather` (`src/core/ledgers/Classifier.ts` at `dd8dee79`) replaced the per-pair quiet and near-set reads, each of which copied a judgment through `JudgmentManager.judgment`. The cached tally, 12956 reads at 800 messages and 50906 at 1600 on both builds (O56), counts only `#ask`'s cached readings and is no copy count. The 3% to 4% share of the rc-a3 profile is retired as stale, not as an under-estimate; the copy share of the committed build's `select` is unknown until rc-u19's before slot reads it, and no claim that the effect exceeds any figure stands.
  - Forecast before any source edit, a stated departure from the bench-first rule that a trigger checks the instrument and never the gain, written into the brief-2 before the pilot: the `judgment(id)` and `judgments()` calls of one correction `select` at 1600 messages are counted before the pilot, by a bench-side counter outside `src/` that replaces no project-owned behavior or derived term by term from the `dd8dee79` code paths and written down, never taken from the cached tally; the forecast is that count times the before build's per-read cost from its micro cell, scaled by the seed's mean serialized judgment length over the micro cell's, plus each whole-array `judgments()` copy, priced from its own cell; the edit is written only when the forecast drop is at least the larger of 16 ms and (q + 0.84) × c_diff(m) × M at the m the pilot fixes, a bar this ruling fixes over the refuters' alternatives (P1's max(3, t) × c_diff and P2's example of 3% of M with 16 ms), and otherwise the unit stops before any source edit and reports, a forecast short only by a term left uncounted, a lower bound, going to the Orchestrator. The tight-loop cost is a lower bound for reads inside a `select`; the forecast is a screen, never the profile share rc-a18 reads, and it decides no keep.
  - m: rc-u19's own correction-arm pilot sets m. § Detectability's 3.8% floor at m = 6 is the control arm's; rc-a3b's after slot read three correction-arm processes of the committed build at 1600 messages with medians of 2133.4, 2122.4, and 2144.7 ms, a between-process spread of 0.52%, and its cost-only slot read c_diff 0.62% at m = 2 on that arm, both pointing to 3% being readable at m = 3. When no m up to 6 reads 3%, m is fixed at 6 as a stated departure from `designs/measurement.md` § Method, MM-9, a contingency, in place of stopping the unit. A slot under the contingency also departs from MM-13. Its A/A point and interval checks stand. In place of MM-13's 3.0% clause, the slot reads `unreadable` when (q + 0.84) × c_diff(m) × M at the slot's re-derived c_b exceeds the forecast drop, so the slot can still show the forecast drop against no change. Its one 2m rerun runs at m = 12, MM-12's doubling, because the cap of 6 bounds the pilot's m and not the doubled rerun.
  - Keep, under O64 and MM-18: the gain reads shown when the 95% interval of the after build's correction-arm `select` at 1600 messages over the before build's, on the last after slot that ran to its end, clears a ratio of 1.0 in the faster direction, and not shown otherwise. The after slot reruns one time, at 2m when the 3% target reads `marginal` or when the point sits under 1.0 with the interval including 1.0, and an `unreadable` slot's rerun is that one rerun; a second `unreadable` slot reads not shown. A shown gain keeps the change, with `meets`, `marginal`, or `misses` against 3% and 16 ms recorded as read; a gain that is not shown reverts it, because rc-u19 is a pure speed change and its brief names no merit beyond speed. A failed no-regression bar or a differing digest reverts it in every case, and a failed bar beside a shown gain is a mixed change the brief rules a revert. Each no-regression bar reads its point only, so no `marginal` reading arises on one. The `judgment(id)` micro cell and the hydrate cells stay out of the keep, a stated departure from TH-6 and from MM-18's case the change targets, because a read of every id repeated is a load no caller produces (`@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Performance); TH-6's 2x reading is recorded beside the keep.
  - U3: `freezeJSON(value: unknown): unknown` in `src/core/cloners.ts` returns a deep-frozen owned JSON copy, or undefined where `copyJSON` returns undefined, and never touches its input: one serialization inside `attempt` and one `JSON.parse` whose reviver freezes each value bottom-up. `@orkestrel/contract`'s `parseJSON` takes no reviver, the semantic difference that admits the global parse (`@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Ecosystem reuse). The constructor stores `requireJudgment(freezeJSON(judgment))`; `#store` copies the input through `copyJSON`, stamps the time onto that owned copy, and stores the frozen copy, one added JSON pass on the write path only, so a value that throws on read keeps its `ConversationError` `'JUDGMENT'` refusal. An in-place freezer is refused (`@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/typescript.md` § Immutability), and no lawful non-public form exists: an implementation file holds one class, a non-trivial helper is exported from its kind file and tested, a pure leaf is never a class method, and every kind file is public through `export *` (`@orkestrel/scaffold` `AGENTS.md` § Design laws).
  - U4, NR3: a `hydrates 1804 judgments` cell over synthetic records of the control seed's judgment count joins rc-u19's bench beside the 51106-record cells, read in the after slot at the control group's m with an A/A of the before build in two process sets, its c_b re-derived from that pair, and MM-3's repetition when its warmup median is under 10 ms. NR3 fails when the after build's median of process medians over the before build's is over 1.05 and the per-construction difference over 4 ms, the page floor after the device factor (`designs/measurement.md` § Budgets); this departs from the bare 1.05 form of O66, O67, and D76, because the Budgets table pairs a relative bar with an absolute floor and the cell times one of the ledger hydrate's three passes, and the 4 ms floor binds for any before median under 80 ms. NR3 is readable only when its A/A passes and 3 × c_diff × the before median is at most the larger of 5% of that median and 4 ms; an unreadable NR3 reruns one time, and a second goes to the Orchestrator. NR3 takes no injected arm and the committed bench no busy-wait route, a stated departure from MM-14, because the cell times the constructor synchronously, so the change sits inside the timed region and MM-17's paced overlap cannot arise. The 51106-record hydrate cell is context only, never NR3's positive control. Both hydrate readings are recorded in milliseconds as context, never as D76's trigger, and the `select` gain is reported beside the hydrate reading, because the eager freeze moves work out of the timed `select` into hydrate.

An arm that under-delivers takes one MM-17 recalibration and one rerun; a second failure, or a failure at full delivery of the arm sized for about 90% power on either arm, voids the instrument, and the unit stops before any source edit for the Orchestrator to rule, never a revert. With two such arms, a sound instrument stops the unit about one time in five; the before slot runs no A/A on the control cell, so that ruling reads the control cell's noise in that slot against the pilot's.

That cell moves into the gate group ahead of the keep it serves, and in that one case it decides this bound and nothing else; every other per-call cell stays report-only.

Every written hydrate-path site standing: B's point decides, and a point over 1.05 reverts all of them, unattributed between two sites. Exactly one of two written hydrate-path sites standing, whichever it is and whatever B read: the bar is read one more time in the follow-on group, on that site's single-site copy against A, m processes each, with no A′ and no arm; the writer supplies both single-site files when both sites are written; only that point decides that site, and B's reading stays a record. No written site standing: the bar decides nothing. O77's wording left two cases with no reading, `isJudgment` reverting with `isSection` kept, and `isSection` reverting with B's point over 1.05, and each reads so.
- O88. The Data-Oriented Design read (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on a read of Richard Fabian's *Data-Oriented Design*, a read-only consultation with proposals G1 to G8, and one refuter per proposal, each proposal as its refuter amended it). G8, G4, and G3 are working rules binding this campaign, cited to the book's node 9 and node 4: `designs/measurement.md` § Method holds them, and `tenets.md` § Briefs holds one line.
  - G8: a unit that adds or changes a pass over messages or judgments states the pass's in and out cardinality (filter, one-to-one, or emission); an emission states its bound and where it applies, naming a host-set or absent bound as such; a bound on a model-paid emission applies before the call; and the unit states whether it removes a superlinear term or a constant factor.
  - G4: a new loop over messages or judgments ships a deterministic pin of what it asks of an observable boundary (judge questions, host callbacks) at the layer that does the work, preferring an exact read history; a count is a screen and decides no keep; only a brief claiming a speed change on a store states its reads per write, each marked counted or derived; a stated forecast sits beside its reading as one line, and a ceiling is never scored as a miss. No retrofit runs over sites `dd8dee79` already pins.
  - G1: a deterministic pre-filter before a paid judge call is sound only when it is conservative for everything the paid step can file. The literal veto does not move, because `amends` on a literal-free pair is the paid gate to a supersession, pinned by `asks pairs using whole names and call arguments, and requires tokens only for amends marks` in `tests/src/core/ledgers/Classifier.test.ts`. One throwaway offline probe, with no model and no GPU, runs scripted corrections at the burst goals and reports eligible asks, literal-free asks split by what admitted them, registry-id asks, and the fileable pairs rc-a11's set would miss, as rc-a11's forecast. rc-a11's brief carries one user question on literal-free `supersedes` filing, stating that a Study replay cannot answer it.
  - G3: an absolute budget at the user's load ends optimisation. rc-a18's brief-3, which replaces its brief-2, reports the 400-message correction and control `select` medians and maximums first, with hydrate beside them, on the tree after rc-u19. The user answered on 2026-11-01 that `select` runs on server and page (Q29, O90), so the rule is written before the run against the server budget, 660 ms for `select` and hydrate per request, with the page served by rc-u22's yield (`designs/measurement.md` § Method, TH-8). While the reading sits under that budget, D67 and D75 do not open on a share trigger alone, and stress sizes read growth for those two. TH-3, Q26, O57's D12 routing, D76, rc-u19, and rc-u20 stand. The rule lapses when a re-read at rc-a9's realistic size, or after a unit changing `select`, crosses the budget.
  - Refuted, with what survives. G2: no cross-request filing table is recorded as D12's shape, because the book's direction is calculable over cached (node 9: "poor form to add state and complexity for the sake of performance until late"), so when rc-a18 shows the read side worth spending on, D12 tries one side-effect-free pass per `select` on the existing `LedgerScan` first, and a cross-request table is proposed only on a repeated-select reading at the page budget with answers to `assign`, the write route, the freshness inputs, store-order replay, and the copy-on-write law; `#plan` and `#recall` reading `input.classification` is a stateless simplification that claims simplicity, about 3% of reads. G5: no standing store-or-derive rule, because `designs/records.md` already says it better; D79 survives. G6: guards are public contracts and the payoff is about 10 ms. G7: no row narrows the repeated question and state, because they are what make the stale check work and a narrower row has no V8 read gain; rc-a9 reports the share of snapshot bytes in `state` and `question` beside its checkpoint bytes.

  - Slot 1, the reviver build, quiet, exit 0 in 1118 s, HEAD `e5071dce`, closure `4563d6b4`, host checks passing, digests replaying: correction `select` 2125.5 to 781.7 ms, 0.3651 [0.3565, 0.3739]; the A/A 0.9930 [0.9683, 1.0182]; control `select` 198.1 to 134.1 ms, 0.6769 [0.6711, 0.6828]; the hydrate of 1804 judgments A 52.6, A′ 53.1, and B 73.7 ms, ratio 1.401, +21.1 ms, which fails 1.05 and 4 ms about five times over; report-only, the hydrate of 51106 judgments 1526 to 2157 ms and its reads 301 to 101 ms. - O125. D22, the control-token fix (the user, 2026-11-02 about 20:00 EST, the briefs' T63, verbatim: "see if you can fix Ollama as well, it's okay to defer it but make sure it is noted and planned for since it is a security issue, at least do it once the GPU work is done."; Orchestrator ruling on one read-only consultation and one refuter, every amendment applied, an amendment winning over the ruling's text; listed for the user to reopen). D22 is a tracked unit with a place in the order: a unit inside rc-ol1, built under its candidate pin before Agent 0.0.35 publishes and released in `@orkestrel/ollama` 0.0.29 after it by the layer order. Agent 0.0.35 is not held.
  - The defect is real within one measured bound. The daemon renders each `/api/chat` string into one prompt and tokenizes that prompt with special-token parsing on, so it cannot tell a template token from the same bytes inside content. The reading is O124's one count: one token, one role, Qwen 3.5 2B on Ollama 0.40.13, with no behaviour arm.
  - The path is a source reading. Qwen 3.5 renders through the daemon's Go renderer, and a template applies only to a model with no renderer (`server/prompt.go` and `model/renderers/qwen35.go` at the Ollama tag v0.40.13; C42, C43). The local config is inferred, because the registry manifest's hash differs from the receipt's digest.
  - Reach, on that source reading and unmeasured: every rendered string is a carrier, on every role. Content on each role passes `mapMessages` byte for byte, and the renderer writes a replayed call's name and each argument key raw. On the page-to-server path `createRelay` refuses a page `system` message and an undeclared tool definition by default, and `messageShape` admits `calls` on any other message, so a page can write a call name or an argument key that fabricates the system turn the relay refuses. Stored ledger text replays through the same path.
  - What a token gives one untrusted author on a small model: a turn boundary the model was trained on, structurally the same as the host's own system turn, so an instruction to treat tool text as data has nothing to hold, a prior turn or an approval can be fabricated in the context, and D31's delimiters would be defeated. What it does not give: a `consequential` call still stops at the gate, because the gate reads no model text (`designs/authority.md` § Protection). Agent never protected the model's choice or its answer, and plain text already moves a 4B model 44.6% of the time (C35). The token raises the ceiling of injection, not its floor.
  - The rule, worded to the mechanism: from 0.0.29 the Ollama provider escapes the tokens in its published list, which covers Qwen's role, turn, tool, and reasoning tokens, on every string it sends that the daemon renders, on every role, the host's `system` text included. Other vocabulary tokens and other model families are not neutralized: `MICA_SPECIAL_TOKENS` holds 13 strings against 33 added tokens in the published Qwen 3.5 2B tokenizer config, none of the missing ones a role boundary, and other families' markers are absent. The list stays unchanged and unrenamed. Uniform escaping leaves the wire making no trust decision, so a host that pastes or loads text into `system` cannot make that mistake (`tenets.md` § Trust), and the daemon's renderer supplies the structure, so no legitimate text needs a raw token.
  - Neutralization reuses `escapeSpecialTokens`, a U+200B after the opening `<` of each listed token. Ordinary text keeps identical bytes, and a request holding a listed token changes by one U+200B per occurrence. A false positive costs boundedly: text that spells a listed token gains an invisible character, which a model can copy into a `write` call. The judge path has accepted that cost since Ollama 0.0.27.
  - Where: `mapMessages` in `@orkestrel/ollama` `src/core/helpers.ts` escapes `message.content`, `call.name`, and each argument key and top-level string value. `format`, `options`, `images`, and `model` do not render into the prompt and stay untouched, and `types.ts` does not change.
  - Measured before built: `qwen35.go` writes tools and nested values through `json.Marshal`, whose default turns `<` and `>` into escape sequences, so one live count arm for a tool description and one for a nested argument read first. When both read as text, the `encode` change and the recursion drop, and a template-rendered family stays a stated limit extended when the package tests one where the arm collapses. A carrier the daemon refuses or does not parse is recorded, and its escape stays only on a merit beyond that reading.

## Deferred

Each item reopens on its trigger and on nothing else.

| Id | Item | Reopen when | Design |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | Cache-safe summarizer prefix | the summary prompt share reaches 5% of the run prompt with 0 cached on rc-a9, or a hosted consumer appears | `designs/compaction.md` |
| D2 | Masking old tool results | a census shows tool messages over 30% of folded estimate tokens | `designs/compaction.md` |
| D3 | Verbatim rule pins across folds | rule retention falls under 0.8 after three folds on the rc-a8 rule-retention instrument, which joins rc-a8 when a unit's gate names it (O75) | `designs/compaction.md` |
| D4 | Loaded skill bodies outside the head, never in a late `system` message | a model-driven reading on the smallest local model, over goals that do not ask for a load, shows at least one distinct skill body loaded per 10 turns; a scripted reading decides nothing, and rc-t1's brief owes the reading on its own driver (O115) | `designs/context.md` |
| D5 | Core repeat stop as a denial bound (`AgentOptions.denials`): at the cap the next turn advertises no tools and the run ends in an answer | the authority rule with a static reason fails to stop repeats in the Study arm | `designs/authority.md` § Deferred |

## Sequence

Every unit starts after Agent 0.0.34 is published and pushed and designs against its names. Size `S` is small, `M` is medium; `run` is a GPU run. Each medium unit gets one independent review. "Gate row" points at `designs/measurement.md` § Gates. A unit that adds, renames, or removes a barrel export owns that symbol's `guides/agent.md` rows, because `test:guides` asserts that the guide documents every barrel export (`tests/guides.test.ts`); rc-d1 owns the guide's prose and the strategy-choice table.

### Phases

| Phase | Step | Units |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | layout and probes; rc-u12 runs after rc-u1 and rc-u2 are accepted and committed, and before rc-p1 (O28) | rc-u12, rc-p1 |

- Before release, run the read-only consultation with one refuter per proposal, a standing step. It carries a surface and model-text diff of everything added since the intuitiveness review (`buildLedgerIndex`, `matchEntities`, `LedgerCorrectionOptions`, `freezeJSON`, the memo stores, rc-u9's index and scope, rc-u22's `LedgerAgentOptions.scheduler`, rc-u23's `matchesJSON`, and rc-u24's `normalizeWord`, `LEDGER_WORD`, and the `keys` and `ranks` of `LedgerIndex`), and it does not replace the falsify round (O71; `tenets.md` § Evaluation).

It commits no `file:` pin, and the consumer wave then re-pins that work to ^0.0.35 (O71).
- Before the publish, prove the candidate in a consumer: name the last Agent commit before the release commit, gates green, after the pre-release consultation, the release visit, and its substitution commit have landed (O95); pin the candidate Agent in Ollama through `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.agents/skills/orkestrel-publish/scripts/swap.ts`, restore it before publish, run Ollama's migrated tests with one real-browser `ask` case over the page-to-Node wire (`agentStreamToFrames`, `Approver`, and the frames are proven only in Node, and no Agent test project runs a browser), and report what did not run (O71). The Ollama visit's brief carries the census of its test set at the 0.0.35 HEAD, its live-case cap, the Ollama release check and the move of its exact `ollama` pin (O72), the move of its `@orkestrel/probe` range before any `prove` run, because the older workspace walk copies `tmp/` (O33), and `OLLAMA_HOST=http://127.0.0.1:1` on every non-service run. Acceptance: each package's typecheck, Ollama's live authority, integration, and page cases, Ollama's strict `frame` (Q19), and the mcp distribution fixture.
- Name the breaking changes in the 0.0.35 release notes: `extractTokens` renamed `extractLiterals` and `LedgerTokenSet` renamed `LiteralSet`, both at the core root (rc-u2); a blank summarizer digest rejected with `ConversationError` `'SUMMARY'` (rc-u1); every denial written to the model in one form, `"<name>" did not run: <note>`, where 0.0.34 wrote `denied by authority`, with the notes of `designs/authority.md` § Denial notes, `SCOPE_NOTE` reading `it is not one of the listed tools. Use a listed tool or answer with what you have.` and `AUTHORITY_NOTES.refused` reading `it was refused. Do not call it again; continue without it.` (rc-a14, rc-u16, O48); an owner record over its line cap dropping its oldest lines first and its rule and correction lines last, where 0.0.34 dropped its most recent lines first (rc-u5, F2); `Selection.briefing` removed; workspace text leaves `system`; `## Pinned` renamed `## Records`; `allowed` replaced by `verdict`; the `consequential` floor; the `deny` tuple widened; a stored `system` role refused at every ingress (`add`, a snapshot hydrate, the job seed, and a selection) with `ConversationError` `'ROLE'` or `'SNAPSHOT'`, `MessageInput.role` narrowed to `ConversationRole`, and `ConversationManager.open` rejecting a stored snapshot that holds one, where 0.0.34 hydrated it and sent the stored `system` message in place (rc-u13, O37); `AgentContextInterface.select` taking the conversation as its first parameter (rc-u14); a second live run on one conversation instance refused with `AgentError` `'CONCURRENCY'`, a ledger's direct run during `respond` included (rc-u14); `createRelay` refusing a page `system` message and an undeclared tool definition by default with status 422 until `RelayOptions.system` or `RelayOptions.tools` admits them (rc-u15); the `ask` arm added to `AgentChunk`, one `ask` chunk per asked call between `usage` and `tool` (rc-u16, O39); and a call whose name no definition advertises that turn denied with the scope note before the authority evaluates it, under every verdict, with a `deny` event that carries no decision, where 0.0.34 passed such a call to the authority's rules in every case and answered an allowed one with `ToolManager`'s own unknown-tool failure, and rc-a14's `ask` passed an asked one to the approver; it breaks only a host that relied on the unknown-tool text or evaluated unknown names (rc-u16, Q15, O45); `matchEntities` taking the alias index that the added builder makes one time per `LedgerRegistry`, in place of the registry, so a caller that passed a registry builds the index first (rc-a3b, O54); a judgment or a message written during a pair-phase await read at the next `classify` call, where 0.0.34 read it at the next rescan of the same call, and a host's `LedgerLookup.read` run one time per tool message for each `#plan` call, `#recall` call, and `select`'s classifier pass, where 0.0.34 ran it again at every rescan, which a host whose `read` has side effects or counts calls observes (rc-a3b, O51; `designs/records.md` § CPU cost and memoization); `JudgmentManager` reads, `add`, and `resolve` returning the stored deep-frozen record where 0.0.34 returned copies. The same frozen records are the judgments that `ConversationInterface.snapshot()` and `LedgerInterface.snapshot().conversation` carry, `MemoryConversationStore.get` hands those snapshots back by reference, and a classifier result whose one resolved judgment supplies `usage` carries that record's frozen `usage` object. So a write to a returned judgment throws in strict-mode code, and so does a host's edit of a snapshot's judgment to migrate or redact it (rc-u19, O51, O54, O55, O70, O89); a judgment record whose `usage` member is present with the value `undefined` refused by `isJudgment`, `isLedgerSnapshot`, and `requireConversationSnapshot`, where 0.0.34 accepted it; JSON never carries that value, so it reaches only a host that passes an in-memory snapshot holding one (rc-u20, O78); and a throw from a provider's framing seam (its parser's `parse` or the provider's `finish`) that races no cancel and is not already a `ProviderError` or `ProviderAbortError` rejecting as `ProviderError` `'PROTOCOL'` with that throw as its `cause`, where 0.0.34 passed it through unchanged, while a throw from `read`, `frame`, or `clear` is unchanged (rc-u17, O47). `AgentContextInterface` gains the required members `skills` and `memos`, so a host's own implementation of the interface adds them; a context from `createAgentContext` or `createAgent` is unaffected, and no related package implements it (rc-u9).
- Name the additions in the same notes, none of them breaking: `LedgerSnapshot`, with `Ledger.snapshot()`, `LedgerOptions.snapshot`, `isLedgerSnapshot`, and the `LedgerErrorCode` `'SNAPSHOT'` (rc-u4); the alias-index builder `buildLedgerIndex` beside `collectRegistry` and its index type `LedgerIndex` beside `LedgerRegistry` (rc-a3b, O54, `dd8dee79`); `LedgerCorrectionOptions` with `LedgerOptions.correction` and `ClassifierOptions.correction`, and the `LedgerError` `'LIMIT'` refusal of a bad correction limit (rc-u6, `e2ec5db5`, O74); `freezeJSON` in the cloners, which returns a deep-frozen owned JSON copy and never changes its input (rc-u19, O70, `9745e36e`); `MemoManager` and `SkillManager`, with their shapes, events, and guards (rc-u7); `MemoStoreInterface`, `MemoSnapshotRow`, `MemoryMemoStore`, `DatabaseMemoStore`, `createMemoryMemoStore`, and `createDatabaseMemoStore` (rc-u8, O81); `ContextError`, with `isContextError` and the `ContextErrorCode` `'SNAPSHOT'` (`c1306929`); `CONVERSATION_ROLES`, `ConversationRole`, `requireRole`, and `requireConversationSnapshot` (rc-u13); `AgentRunOptions.conversation`, `requireConversation`, and `isConversation`, and a run bound to the conversation active at its call, a defect fix (rc-u14); `RelaySystemOptions`, `RelayToolOptions`, `RelayRefusalReason`, `RelayRefusal`, `REFUSED_RELAY_STATUS`, `REFUSED_RELAY_CONTENT_TYPE`, `buildRelayRequest`, and `describeRelayRefusal` (rc-u15); and the "Page to Node agent" wire contract, `AgentFrame`, `AgentPrompt`, and `AgentAnswer` with their shapes and contracts, `tokenUsageShape`, the frame helpers, `renderArguments`, and `Approver`, `ApproverInterface` with `ask` as a bound `AuthorityHandler` property, so `ask: approver.ask` wires it, and `createApprover` for live `ask` frames (rc-u16, O48); `AgentRunOptions.prompt`, checked by one guarded statement in `Agent#stream` before the run's guards and adding no public name (O49), so a run appends its own user turn and a server fence neither appends nor removes it (rc-u16, Q20, O48); and `RelayProviderOptions.parser` made optional, a relay response framing through a fresh strict `@orkestrel/ndjson` parser when no factory is supplied, with the runtime dependency `@orkestrel/ndjson` ^0.0.15 (rc-u17, Q17). `AgentContextOptions.skills` and `AgentContextOptions.memos`; `AgentOptions.skills` and `AgentOptions.memos`; `ScopeFilter.skills` and `ScopeFilter.memos`, read by `Scope` and its `narrow` method; `LedgerAgentOptions` widened with `instructions`, `skills`, `memos`, and `workspaces`, so one memo set can serve many ledgers and agents of one principal (rc-u9, O48). `AgentContext#build` renders the skills index in the `system` message after the instructions and the memos index in the `user` context message before the workspace text, each as one block with one `- name: description` line per entry; an empty or scoped-out manager renders no header. An index with no loader tool is inert for the model: Agent ships none, and a host that adds skills registers its own (rc-u9, O48). `matchesJSON`, a pure helper that reports whether two values' JSON texts parse to the same JSON value, and false when either side has no JSON text, under this name unless rc-u23's reviewer finds a collision (rc-u23, O111).
- Name the fix in the same notes: A ledger's plan prices the built head, its instructions, skills index, memos index, and workspace text included, plus the request copy with any workspace image, and its calibration pass sends that head. A ledger with no libraries prices and sends the same bytes (rc-u9, `designs/records.md` § Head pricing).
- Name the guard change in the same notes: `isJudgment` and `isMemo` are guard constants since rc-u20, so each reads an empty `.name`, where `isJudgment` was a named function in 0.0.34; a host that logs a guard's name reads an empty string (read on the built bundle, 2026-11-02).
- Name rc-u23's changed `resolve` contract among the breaking changes of the same notes, at its commit: a judgment matches its question by JSON value where 0.0.34 compared JSON text with its key order kept, so a question whose keys arrive in another order, from a store that returns keys sorted or a host that builds it in another order, reuses the recorded judgment where 0.0.34 asked again, and a changed criterion, a changed state, or a changed source order still asks again. Criteria order reaches the prompt and a small model reads options by position, so a reused answer keeps the reading its first order earned, and `remove(id)` asks again after a reorder. Its stated limits: a structured `state` keeps its text compare, key order included; a restored lookup call's arguments render in the order the store returned, so a records line, an item the `recall` tool lists, a lookup stub in the tail, and the provider request can differ in bytes from the ones the original run sent, and a stub's `hidden` form can tell the model to recall with another string argument, because it names the first string in that order, the limit rc-u23's review added to `guides/agent.md`; `computeReading` can name another winner on an exact tie in a restored answer, where `Classifier#category` follows `LEDGER_CATEGORIES` whatever the stored order; and `matchesJSON` is bounded to JSON data and promises nothing for a cycle, a `toJSON` object, `NaN`, an infinity, or an `undefined`, function, symbol, or hole inside a list (rc-u23, `a82c5985`, O111, O112).
- Name the stored rows in the same notes and in rc-d1's upgrade note, at rc-u23's commit: a stored 0.0.34 conversation row matches in any key order after upgrade, and a record dead under the text compare becomes live, with no migration (rc-u23, O110).
- Name rc-u22 in the same notes, at `77df10c2`, among the additions: `LedgerAgentOptions` gains `scheduler`, `ClassifierOptions` gains `scheduler`, and `LEDGER_SLICE` sets the 5 milliseconds a paced classifier files between host turns; the `skipped` outcome also covers a question whose message left the conversation. A page stays responsive only when it passes `agent: { scheduler: createBrowserScheduler() }`, and a page that omits it runs each stretch of cached filing as one task, with a host turn only while the classifier waits on the judge (O90, TH-9).
- Name rc-u24 in the same notes, at `c70e64cd`: `LedgerIndex` gains `keys` and `ranks`, beside rc-a3b's `matchEntities` entry among the breaking changes; `normalizeWord` and `LEDGER_WORD` are additions; `matchEntities` keeps its signature, members, and order, with its two stated limits, U+0345 written for an iota and a name holding no letter or digit run, and the fold is proven on Node 22's Unicode data, a browser's tables unread (D86). A review finding outside its claims enters this line when it would change a selection's bytes, drop a match today's code finds beyond the stated limits, or slow a case users hit past its bar (rc-u24, Q31).
- Name rc-u25 in the same notes, at `275c0dab`: a ledger's `select` no longer renders once per dropped line, with selection bytes the same on every seed it read and no public type change; `findLedgerCut` and `buildLedgerCut` are additions (O123).
- Carry the trust note in the same notes and in rc-d1: Skill text renders in the `system` message, so a host builds each `SkillInput` from text it authored; a page's `system` message, its skills index included, is refused by `createRelay` until `RelaySystemOptions.accept` admits it (rc-u9, rc-u15, O73).
- Name the upgrade path for data written before 0.0.35 in the same notes and in rc-d1's upgrade note: a stored snapshot that holds a `system` row is refused, and rc-u18's stored-row hint says to remove the row before loading; a queued `AgentJobInput` seed that holds a `system` message fails at `AgentRegistry.build` through `add` with rc-u18's ingress hint, and the job's `system` member carries that text (rc-u13, rc-u18, O49).
- rc-ct1 closed as a reverted miss, so no `@orkestrel/contract` release follows from it and nothing re-pins: contract's source stays at the 0.0.21 bytes, and its span pins and bench sit in the local commit `09d0673` (Q25, Q28, O51, O62).
- The scaffold law amendment of Q24 is the scaffold session's, carried by `@orkestrel/scaffold` 0.0.123, which the user publishes; no unit of this campaign waits on it, and briefs cite `.claude/rules/tests.md` § Probes for the bench law until Agent's visit to that release re-cites it (O51, O54).
- Carry D22 in the 0.0.35 notes and in rc-d1 as a stated limit: a provider decides how message text reaches its model; from `@orkestrel/ollama` 0.0.29 the Ollama provider escapes the tokens in its published list, which covers Qwen's role, turn, tool, and reasoning tokens; other vocabulary tokens and other model families are not neutralized. Before 0.0.29, a listed token inside any message reaches Ollama's renderer as the real token. Agent 0.0.35 is not held and changes no core code (O125). rc-ol1's pre-publish proof runs on the escape's final bytes, and Ollama 0.0.29's release commit body names the escape with its stated limits.
- The toolbox wave carries rc-t1 only and waits for D20.
